### SOURCE, PROGRESSION AND CURRENT-LINKAGE AUDIT

- **Generation date:** 2026-09-02.
- **Repository-first evidence:** the Basic owner is taught first and preserved in full; the Advanced owner is retained only in the optional final teaching block.
- **OCR evidence:** Repository Markdown was primary. The OCR-searchable official General Studies question papers held under books\mains and books\more_previous_papers were read only to confirm the printed wording of routed Mains demands. No official answer key, marking scheme, page precision or unsupported quotation was imported from them.
- **Qdrant:** not used; repository Markdown and available OCR context were sufficient.
- **PYQ integrity:** The audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner for the covered years, and that zero is reported openly rather than concealed or filled with a manufactured demand card. The four audited ledgers consulted are the Mains routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025, and neither the Basic owner nor the Advanced owner for this topic carries a generated previous-year integration block, which is itself consistent with that zero. Both owners state independently that there is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question specifically on the other backward classes or the economically weaker sections in the audited local previous-year texts, and this package adopts exactly that position. The two nearest neighbouring demands are the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from a statutory to a constitutional body, and the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger routes both to the Polity commissions owner together with the consolidated body-design owner, so no ownership is claimed over either here; reservation doctrine such as the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling and the constitutional validity reasoning for the economic criterion is likewise routed to the Polity special-provisions owner and is cross-linked rather than duplicated. The practice in this package therefore consists of six original Mains questions with model solutions, each written to the repository's examiner-grade standard using the claim, named evidence, analysis and qualification pattern, together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A → B → C → D rotation. Each model solution opens with its analytical claim so that the evidence which follows does argumentative rather than decorative work. The locally held OCR-searchable official General Studies papers were read only to confirm that no question on this owner's subject matter is routed here; no question was invented from them, no marking scheme or page precision was imported and no official answer key is claimed for any question anywhere in this package.
- **Live-link boundary:** One live official page was verified for this topic on 2026-09-02. The National Commission for Backward Classes About Us page on the commission's own website, read on that date, records that the commission was initially constituted by the central government under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993 dated 2 April 1993 and had so far been reconstituted seven times up to 2016; that the Act of 1993 has been repealed through the National Commission for Backward Classes (Repeal) Act, 2018 dated 14 August 2018; that the present eighth commission has been accorded constitutional status and was constituted through the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 dated 11 August 2018, whereby Article 338B was inserted, forming a commission for the socially and educationally backward classes to be known as the National Commission for Backward Classes; and that the commission consists of a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and three other Members in the rank and pay of Secretary to the Government of India, whose conditions of service and tenure of office were notified by a Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment notification dated 23 August 2018. Those statements are used only for what they say, and no case, complaint, recommendation, sitting or list-revision figure is inferred from them. Four further official checks were attempted on the same date, namely a departmental scheme identifier that returned a not-found page, a second departmental scheme identifier that returned the same not-found page, a departmental scheme listing page that returned navigational content only and a departmental sector-information page that returned navigational content only, so nothing was read or used from them and no secondary summary, coaching site or news report has been treated as an official source. The package therefore relies on that verified page together with the dated official anchors already carried by the repository owners, each cited with its issuing instrument or decision date: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) for special provision and reservation; Article 342A for the Central List, with the President specifying and Parliament alone varying it; Article 338B for the national commission; the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021, the second of which restored the power of States to prepare their own lists for State purposes after the 2021 Maratha reservation reading; the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserting Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) for reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections outside the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes and the other backward classes; the decision in Indra Sawhney against Union of India of 1992 upholding twenty-seven per cent reservation, mandating the creamy-layer exclusion for the other backward classes only and stating the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling; the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling of eight lakh rupees notified in September 2017, applied over three consecutive years on top of notified parental service and status categories and with salary and agricultural-land income excluded from simple clubbing; the Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019 setting a gross annual family income below eight lakh rupees together with the asset limits of five acres of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft, a residential plot of 100 sq yards in a notified municipality and 200 sq yards elsewhere; the decision in Janhit Abhiyan against Union of India of November 2022 upholding that reservation by a majority of three to two, with the recorded majority reasoning and the recorded dissent; the umbrella education-support framework PM-YASASVI of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students of the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes; the Rohini Commission of 2017 examining sub-categorisation, with implementation recorded as pending as of mid-2026; the last published all-caste census tables of 1931 and the unreleased and unvalidated caste data of the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011; and the recorded status as at 21 July 2026 that the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 to include caste enumeration, that the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise and that the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026. No population share, beneficiary count, certificate count, seat-fill rate, scholarship disbursement, budget or expenditure figure is asserted, no National Family Health Survey, Periodic Labour Force Survey, National Sample Survey, NITI Aayog or Sustainable Development Goal value is asserted, no legal or constitutional provision is invented or upgraded in status, no judicial observation is converted into a binding holding or a legislated rule, no pending report or bill is described as enacted or implemented, and no previous-year question, official answer key or option is asserted or inferred.
- **Fact/inference discipline:** no current-affairs item, PYQ wording, figure or quotation is invented.

### EVIDENCE, PYQ AND CURRENT-STATUS CONTROL

- **Prohibition/outcome discipline:** a legal prohibition, reported case, registration, conviction, rehabilitation measure and lived eradication are different claims.
- **Eligibility discipline:** universal, categorical, means-tested, self-declared, notified-list and residence-linked routes require exact scope; coverage never proves adequacy or access.
- **Reservation discipline:** constitutional basis, beneficiary category, competent list, ceiling, creamy-layer rule, EWS route, horizontal/vertical operation and judicial status are qualified separately.
- **Fiscal discipline:** allocation, release, expenditure, unit cost, beneficiary transfer and outcome are not interchangeable.
- **Data discipline:** retain numerator, denominator, unit, geography, reference period, release date, provisional/final status and survey-versus-administrative character.
- **Causal discipline:** headline change, before-after sequence or cross-state correlation does not establish scheme impact without mechanism and counterfactual limits.
- **PYQ discipline:** exact wording is preserved only when verified; routed or reconstructed demands remain labelled and no model is presented as an official UPSC answer.
- **Current-status note, rechecked 2026-09-06:** volatile law, scheme, judgment, list, budget and dataset claims retain issuing authority, date and operative/interim/final status.

**Generation-local live/current sources:**

### SESSION 1 — FOUNDATION — What the backward-class owner holds and how it is examined

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** What the backward-class owner holds and how it is examined is the part of OBC, EWS and Social Mobility that explains how What this owner holds and why its previous-year record is stated as zero fit into one examinable social-justice relationship.

**Technical definition:** In social-justice analysis, What the backward-class owner holds and how it is examined denotes the source-bounded relation among What this owner holds and why its previous-year record is stated as zero, described by the entitlement or claim that is asserted, the legal, statutory or purely administrative form that carries it, the delivery mechanism that is supposed to convert it into access, the measured outcome by which the conversion is tested, and the remedy or recommendation that follows when the conversion fails.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- The two commission demands of 2018 and 2022 are routed to the Polity commissions owner and are not claimed here.

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- The backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are constitutionally distinct enabling provisions.

### SESSION 3 — FOUNDATION — The Central List route and the State list route

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The Central List route and the State list route is the part of OBC, EWS and Social Mobility that explains how The Central List route under Article 342A and State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment fit into one examinable social-justice relationship.

**Technical definition:** In social-justice analysis, The Central List route and the State list route denotes the source-bounded relation among The Central List route under Article 342A and State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment, described by the entitlement or claim that is asserted, the legal, statutory or purely administrative form that carries it, the delivery mechanism that is supposed to convert it into access, the measured outcome by which the conversion is tested, and the remedy or recommendation that follows when the conversion fails.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Central List route and the State list route should be analysed as a relation among The Central List route under Article 342A and State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment, separating the right claimed, the scheme that carries it, the delivery that reaches a person and the outcome that is actually measured.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **Central**
- **List**
- **State**
- **Article**
- **lists**
- **restoration**

**How to use them:** Define Central, List, State; cite Article as named evidence with its issuing body and dated edition or round; then apply this qualification: The 105th Amendment restored a State competence that a judicial reading of the 102nd Amendment had removed. Conclude by showing how the distinction supports this answer route: Narrate a list-making controversy in its exact amendment and judgment sequence..

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
THE CENTRAL LIST ROUTE AND THE STATE LIST ROUTE
01. The Central List route under Article 342A
    |
    v
02. State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment
BOUNDARY -> The 105th Amendment restored a State competence that a judicial reading of the 102nd Amendment had removed.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation. State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
- State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- The 105th Amendment restored a State competence that a judicial reading of the 102nd Amendment had removed.

#### EXAM LINK

- **Prelims:** Retain the exact statute, section, article, ministry, official category, survey round or dated edition attached to The Central List route and the State list route, and never upgrade a policy document into a statute or a targeted scheme into a universal entitlement.
- **Mains:** Narrate a list-making controversy in its exact amendment and judgment sequence.

#### MINI RECAP

- **Evidence chain:** The Central List route under Article 342A -> State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment
- **Qualified use:** Narrate a list-making controversy in its exact amendment and judgment sequence.

### SESSION 5 — CORE — The creamy layer as a status-and-income filter

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The creamy layer as a status-and-income filter is the part of OBC, EWS and Social Mobility that explains how The creamy layer is a status test before it is an income test and Creamy layer is confined to the other backward classes fit into one examinable social-justice relationship.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The owners define the creamy-layer exclusion as a rule resting primarily on the notified parental service and status categories, with an income and wealth test applying where relevant at the notified ceiling of eight lakh rupees measured over three consecutive years, and they add the exclusion that salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; the reason the composition of the rule matters is that a candidate who compresses it into the single sentence that a family income above ₹8 lakh disqualifies an applicant has stated neither the parental status limb nor the income exclusions, and will therefore misread every close option built on the difference between a status ground and an income ground. The owners state without qualification that the creamy-layer exclusion applies to reservation for the other backward classes and does not apply to reservation for the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes, and they attribute that position to the decision in Indra Sawhney against Union of India and the jurisprudence that followed it; the trap this fact defuses is the very common transfer of an other-backward-class filter to a Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe answer, and the corresponding trap in the opposite direction, which is to assume that because a category is listed it must carry the same internal filter as every other listed category.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Janhit Abhiyan against Union of India, decided in November 2022 by a majority of three to two, upheld the economically weaker sections reservation, the majority reasoning that an economic criterion alone can be a basis for affirmative action and that going beyond the fifty per cent ceiling does not violate the basic structure when the addition creates a new category rather than enlarging the existing reserved categories, while the dissent argued that an economic criterion cannot be used to exclude the existing reserved categories from a new benefit without violating substantive equality; the owners require both limbs to be stated, because reciting only the outcome converts a closely divided constitutional argument into a settled consensus.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The owners record that the last published all-caste census tables are from 1931 and that the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011 collected caste data which was never officially released or validated for general policy use, and they draw the consequence explicitly: without a published population dataset it is not possible to say whether reservation proportions match population proportions, which sub-groups within the category are actually benefiting, or how outcomes move across cohorts; the discipline for an answer is that this is an evidence gap and not a claim about the size of any community, so no share, count or proportion for the other backward classes may be asserted from it. The owners carry the status of the enumeration decision with unusual care as at 21 July 2026: the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 that caste enumeration will be included, the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise, and the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026; the owners then state the boundary, which is that this is an approved and ongoing operation and not published caste data, not a completed methodology and not an automatic basis for revising any list or validating any sub-categorisation formula, so an answer may use the decision as a forthcoming evidence source and must not use it as evidence.

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it. The owners state the analytical core of the topic as an identification paradox: reservation for the other backward classes is premised on social and educational backwardness, yet the absence of a published population dataset since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions, evidence-based sub-categorisation and any cohort-level tracking of mobility, so the policy operates without the feedback loop that would tell it whether it is working; the owners are careful that this is a claim about the evidence base and not a claim that the policy has failed, and an answer should use it to argue for measurement design rather than to assert an outcome that the data cannot support.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it.

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome. The audited routing ledgers, which cover the Mains papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025, route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner, and both the Basic and the Advanced owner state independently that no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question on this subject matter exists in the audited local texts, which is consistent with that zero; the 2022 General Studies Paper II demand on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status is routed to the Polity commissions owner and the 2018 General Studies Paper II demand on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions is routed there as well, so neither is claimed here, and the practice in this package is therefore six original Mains questions with model solutions written to the repository's examiner-grade standard together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A-B-C-D rotation.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

#### COMPLETE BASIC OWNER EVIDENCE BANK

> **Subject:** Social Justice | **Tier:** Must-Do (foundation) | **GS Paper:** GS-II;
> cross-cuts GS-I (society, mobility) and GS-III (inclusive growth).
> **Core area:** Other Backward Classes, Economically Weaker Sections, creamy layer,
> social mobility, Central/State OBC lists, PM-YASASVI.
> **Grounded in:** National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993; 102nd and 105th
> Constitutional Amendment Acts; 103rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019 (EWS); Indra
> Sawhney v. Union of India (1992); Janhit Abhiyan v. Union of India (2022); PM-YASASVI
> scheme guidelines; Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment.
> ✅ = source-grounded | ⚠️ = analytical inference | 📰 = current anchor.
> *Companion: `advanced/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md`.*

##### 1. Visual foundation

```text
               BACKWARD-CLASS WELFARE ARCHITECTURE
                              |
       ------------------------------------------------
       |                      |                       |
  IDENTIFICATION         RESERVATION              WELFARE
       |                      |                       |
  Central List           OBC: 27%                PM-YASASVI
  (President ->          (central jobs           (notified education
   Parliament varies;    & institutions)          support routes)
   NCBC advice)
       |                      |                   post-matric,
  State Lists            EWS: 10%
  (state govts           (103rd Amdt,                 |
   post-105th            non-SC/ST/OBC)         OUTCOME TEST
   Amdt, 2021)                |                       |
       |                 Creamy layer            SOCIAL MOBILITY
       +---------- excludes well-off  ------------> intergenerational
                   OBCs from reservation           uplift?
```

**Core proposition:** ✅ OBC reservation addresses historical social and educational
backwardness; EWS reservation addresses economic disadvantage among non-reserved
categories. Together, they aim for substantive equality, but their impact must be
measured through intergenerational social mobility — not merely scheme coverage.

##### 2. Essential definitions

| Concept | Exam-ready meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ **Other Backward Classes (OBC)** | Socially and educationally backward classes of citizens other than Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes, identified on the basis of social and educational backwardness (not solely economic) and entitled to reservation under Articles 15(4) and 16(4). |
| ✅ **Central OBC List** | The list of socially and educationally backward classes for central purposes under Article 342A. The President specifies the Central List and Parliament alone may include or exclude a community; NCBC consultation/advice is part of the institutional process, not a power to add a community by itself. |
| ✅ **State OBC Lists** | Lists maintained by state governments for state-level reservations; after the 105th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2021, states regained explicit power to prepare their own OBC/SEBC lists for state purposes. |
| ✅ **Creamy layer** | An OBC exclusion rule based primarily on the parents' service/status categories and, where the income/wealth test applies, the notified ₹8 lakh ceiling for three consecutive years. Salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; do not reduce the rule to “family income above ₹8 lakh”. |
| ✅ **Economically Weaker Sections (EWS)** | A category introduced by the 103rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019, enabling up to 10% reservation in education and public employment for economically weaker citizens who do not fall under SC/ST/OBC categories, based on a gross annual family income below ₹8 lakh and specified asset limits (notified January 2019). |
| ⚠️ **Social mobility** | Intergenerational movement in educational attainment, occupational status or income; the ultimate test of whether backward-class welfare converts into durable uplift, not merely first-generation access. |

##### 3. How OBC/EWS reservation and welfare work (mechanism)

1. **OBC identification:** For the Central List, the constitutional Article 342A route
   applies (President specifies; Parliament varies); NCBC has consultative/advisory and
   safeguards functions. State governments maintain their own lists for State purposes
   after the 105th Amendment.
2. **Creamy-layer filter:** Before claiming OBC reservation, an applicant must obtain
   a certificate verifying they do not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion criteria
   (income ceiling and parental office threshold, periodically revised).
3. **Reservation implementation:** 27% reservation for OBCs in central services and
   central educational institutions; 10% EWS reservation (post-2019) for non-SC/ST/OBC
   economically weaker citizens — see Polity for constitutional-doctrine detail.
4. **Welfare schemes:** PM-YASASVI bundles support for OBC, EBC and DNT students,
   including pre-/post-matric and top-class education routes and other notified
   components. Check the applicable annual guideline rather than assuming every
   component is open in every State or year.
5. **Social mobility as outcome test:** The ultimate goal is intergenerational uplift —
   children of beneficiaries achieving higher education and occupation than their
   parents — rather than static coverage numbers.

##### 4. Institutions and tools

- ✅ **National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC):** A constitutional body (post-
  102nd Amendment, 2018) with mandate to examine inclusion/exclusion requests and
  recommend changes to the Central OBC List; advises on policy and grievances. For
  detailed composition and constitutional mandate, see `Polity/basic/National-Commissions-SC-ST-BC.md`.
- ✅ **105th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2021:** Restored states' power to prepare
  their own OBC/SEBC lists for state-level reservation after the Supreme Court in the
  Maratha reservation case (2021) held that only Parliament could do so under the
  102nd Amendment's wording; this Act clarified the President's list binds only
  central purposes, freeing states for state purposes.
- ✅ **103rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019:** Inserted Articles 15(6) and 16(6)
  enabling up to 10% EWS reservation; upheld by the Supreme Court in Janhit Abhiyan v.
  Union of India (November 2022) by a 3-2 majority. For constitutional-validity
  reasoning, see `Polity/basic/Special-Provisions.md`.
- ✅ **PM-YASASVI (Young Achievers' Scholarship Award Scheme for Vibrant India):**
  umbrella support for OBC, EBC and DNT students under the Ministry of Social Justice
  and Empowerment. Its scholarship/coaching components and State implementation should
  be identified from the current guideline; EBC is not interchangeable with the
  constitutional EWS category.
- ⚠️ **EWS certificate:** Issued by state/UT authorities certifying that the applicant's
  family meets the income and asset criteria for EWS reservation — an administrative
  verification challenge given income-based (vs caste-based) identification.

##### 5. Indian applications and examples

- ⚠️ A first-generation college graduate from an OBC family who becomes a professional
  and whose child enters a top-tier institution illustrates successful intergenerational
  social mobility — the intended outcome, not merely reservation-slot uptake.
- ⚠️ The creamy-layer exclusion prevents the benefits of OBC reservation from being
  captured repeatedly by the already-advanced section within the OBC category.
- ⚠️ EWS reservation faced the challenge of income-based identification — verifying
  annual income is administratively harder than verifying caste-based certificates,
  raising exclusion and fraud risks.

##### 6. Must-Know Facts for Prelims

- ✅ NCBC is a constitutional body (post-102nd Amendment, 2018); earlier it was a
  statutory body under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993.
- ✅ 27% OBC reservation in central services/institutions; 10% EWS reservation (post-
  103rd Amendment, 2019) for non-SC/ST/OBC economically weaker citizens.
- ✅ Creamy-layer exclusion applies to OBC reservation; it does NOT apply to SC/ST
  reservation.
- ✅ The OBC creamy-layer income/wealth ceiling is ₹8 lakh (last revised September
  2017), but its parental service/status categories and exclusions for salary/agricultural
  income matter. The EWS criterion also uses ₹8 lakh plus asset limits, but the two
  tests are legally different.
- ✅ The 105th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2021 restored states' power to prepare
  their own OBC lists for state-level reservation.
- ✅ PM-YASASVI covers OBC, EBC and DNT students with pre-matric, post-matric and
  top-class education scholarships.

##### 7. UPSC traps

- ❌ Creamy layer applies to SC/ST reservation. -> ✅ Creamy layer applies ONLY to OBC
  reservation; SC/ST reservation has no creamy-layer exclusion as per the Indra Sawhney
  judgment and subsequent jurisprudence.
- ❌ The NCBC can directly add communities to the Central OBC List. -> ✅ NCBC only
  recommends; Parliament must act on the recommendation to include/exclude communities
  from the Central List.
- ❌ EWS reservation is for economically weaker OBCs. -> ✅ EWS reservation under the
  103rd Amendment is for economically weaker citizens who are NOT SC/ST/OBC; OBCs have
  their separate 27% reservation.
- ❌ States cannot prepare their own OBC lists after the 102nd Amendment. -> ✅ The
  105th Amendment (2021) explicitly restored states' power to prepare their own OBC
  lists for state-level reservation purposes.

##### 8. 📰 Current anchor

- 📰 **Census 2027 status (21 July 2026):** the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs
  decided on 30 April 2025 that caste enumeration will be included. Census 2027 is
  planned as an all-digital exercise; the first-phase houselisting window was notified
  for 1 April–30 September 2026. This is an approved, ongoing operation — not published
  caste data, a completed methodology or an automatic basis for revising OBC lists.
- ✅ The OBC income/wealth ceiling is ₹8 lakh (DoPT, September 2017), but quote the full
  creamy-layer test — parental status/service categories and income exclusions — rather
  than calling it a simple gross-family-income limit.

##### 9. PYQ application

- ⚠️ There is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 GS-II Mains PYQ specifically on OBC/EWS
  in the audited local PYQ texts. However, the topic intersects with broader social-
  justice and reservation questions. A strong Mains answer should distinguish OBC
  (social-educational backwardness) from EWS (economic backwardness), explain the
  creamy-layer rationale, cite the 2022 Janhit Abhiyan judgment for EWS, and link
  reservation to social-mobility outcomes.

##### 10. Mains angles

- ⚠️ Frame OBC reservation as addressing caste-based social and educational backwardness,
  and EWS reservation as addressing economic disadvantage among non-reserved categories.
- ⚠️ Use the creamy-layer mechanism to explain intra-group equity within OBC reservation.
- ⚠️ Distinguish coverage (reservation slots filled, scholarships disbursed) from outcome
  (intergenerational mobility, educational/occupational progression).
- ⚠️ The caste-data gap remains a policy lacuna: the 2027 enumeration decision is
  approved and operations have begun, but results, validation and any policy use are
  still unavailable.

> **Answer thesis:** OBC reservation addresses historical social-educational backwardness
> with a creamy-layer filter to ensure intra-group equity, while EWS reservation
> addresses pure economic disadvantage among non-reserved categories; their success
> must be judged not by coverage statistics alone but by intergenerational social
> mobility. Census 2027 may later improve the evidence base, but a decision to enumerate
> caste is not yet usable population evidence.

##### 11. Probable questions

- ⚠️ **Prelims:** Which constitutional amendment restored states' power to prepare their
  own OBC lists for state-level reservation purposes?
- ⚠️ **Mains (10 marks):** Distinguish OBC reservation from EWS reservation with reference
  to the constitutional basis, identification criteria and judicial pronouncements.
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** "Reservation is a means to social mobility, not an end in
  itself." Critically examine this statement in the context of backward-class welfare
  policy in India.

##### 11A. Answer architecture (10/15/20-mark support)

- **Doctrine:** *Indra Sawhney* on OBC reservation, creamy layer and ordinary 50% ceiling;
  102nd/105th Amendments on NCBC and State lists; *Janhit Abhiyan* upheld EWS reservation.
- **Sub-classification:** Rohini Commission examined unequal OBC benefit distribution;
  internal classification needs credible data and safeguards against capture.
- **Mobility chain:** identification -> capabilities -> entry -> retention/promotion ->
  assets and social acceptance.

**10 marks:** distinguish OBC and EWS criteria. **15 marks:** doctrine, data, creamy
layer, sub-classification and design. **20 marks:** compensatory discrimination, poverty
relief, representation, federalism and intra-group inequality.

> **Reasoned verdict:** Affirmative action needs fair internal distribution and
> complementary capability investment to create durable mobility.

##### 12. Study links

- ✅ Advanced companion: `advanced/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md`.
- ✅ `Polity/basic/Special-Provisions.md` — constitutional reservation doctrine, 103rd
  Amendment EWS validity (Janhit Abhiyan), and 50% ceiling jurisprudence.
- ✅ `Polity/basic/National-Commissions-SC-ST-BC.md` — NCBC constitutional status,
  composition and mandate.
- ✅ `basic/07_Scheduled-Castes-Rights-Atrocities-and-Welfare.md` — SC reservation and
  welfare architecture, for comparison with OBC/EWS targeting.

### SOCIAL JUSTICE DEEP-REVIEW CORE CONTROL

- **Must remember:** OBC, EWS and social mobility analysis separates social and educational backwardness, economic disadvantage, representation, capability, discrimination and intergenerational mobility across different constitutional and policy routes.
- **Close distinction:** OBC is not a synonym for poverty, EWS is not an OBC sub-category, creamy-layer doctrine does not travel identically across every reservation category, a commission recommendation is not automatic inclusion, and mobility is not proof that structural barriers disappeared.
- **Authority / evidence / implementation limit:** Use Articles 15, 16 and 342A, NCBC, list competence and judicial ceilings/qualifications precisely; map identification, data, notification, eligibility, certificate, grievance, representation and outcomes while avoiding unqualified quota or fiscal claims.

### Semantic-completeness ownership and PYQ control

- **Official syllabus/index and owned core:** OBC, EWS and social mobility analysis separates social and educational backwardness, economic disadvantage, representation, capability, discrimination and intergenerational mobility across different constitutional and policy routes.
- **Indispensable distinction and prerequisite taxonomy:** OBC is not a synonym for poverty, EWS is not an OBC sub-category, creamy-layer doctrine does not travel identically across every reservation category, a commission recommendation is not automatic inclusion, and mobility is not proof that structural barriers disappeared.
- **Mechanism, implementation and evidence control:** Use Articles 15, 16 and 342A, NCBC, list competence and judicial ceilings/qualifications precisely; map identification, data, notification, eligibility, certificate, grievance, representation and outcomes while avoiding unqualified quota or fiscal claims.
- **✅ Verified current fact (official sources rechecked 6 September 2026):**
  Rechecked 2026-09-06: the Department, NCBC and Constitution remain authoritative; OBC identification, EWS eligibility, list competence, creamy-layer doctrine and mobility evidence follow distinct legal and analytical routes. Sources: https://socialjustice.gov.in/; https://www.ncbc.nic.in/; https://legislative.gov.in/constitution-of-india/
- **⚠️ Analytical inference:** institutional design, allocation, a portal, a
  registration, a report, a training completion, a disposal count or a ranking
  can support a causal argument only after authority, capacity, incentives,
  distribution, implementation, grievance, outcome and alternative explanations
  are tested.
- **Canonical and cross-owner boundary:** this Social Justice owner teaches the
  authority-to-delivery-to-remedy chain. Detailed constitutional doctrine stays
  with Polity; sector entitlement design stays with Social Justice; macro-fiscal
  doctrine stays with Economy; ethics theory stays with Ethics. Cross-owner
  evidence may be routed but is not silently duplicated or re-owned.
- **Four-ledger hostile audit:** literal syllabus, indispensable prerequisites,
  standard public-administration/social justice taxonomy and complete verified PYQ
  demands were checked for absent concepts, institutions, mechanisms,
  classifications, exceptions, comparisons, criticisms, current status,
  answer architecture and dependent artifacts.
- **Verified PYQ ownership, 2018-2026:** The audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner for the covered years, and that zero is reported openly rather than concealed or filled with a manufactured demand card. The four audited ledgers consulted are the Mains routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025, and neither the Basic owner nor the Advanced owner for this topic carries a generated previous-year integration block, which is itself consistent with that zero. Both owners state independently that there is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question specifically on the other backward classes or the economically weaker sections in the audited local previous-year texts, and this package adopts exactly that position. The two nearest neighbouring demands are the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from a statutory to a constitutional body, and the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger routes both to the Polity commissions owner together with the consolidated body-design owner, so no ownership is claimed over either here; reservation doctrine such as the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling and the constitutional validity reasoning for the economic criterion is likewise routed to the Polity special-provisions owner and is cross-linked rather than duplicated. The practice in this package therefore consists of six original Mains questions with model solutions, each written to the repository's examiner-grade standard using the claim, named evidence, analysis and qualification pattern, together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A-B-C-D rotation. Each model solution opens with its analytical claim so that the evidence which follows does argumentative rather than decorative work. The locally held OCR-searchable official General Studies papers were read only to confirm that no question on this owner's subject matter is routed here; no question was invented from them, no marking scheme or page precision was imported and no official answer key is claimed for any question anywhere in this package.

## BASIC MCQS / REMEDIATION

### Q1. Which statement correctly identifies What this owner holds and why its previous-year record is stated as zero?

A. This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.
B. The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.
C. For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
D. State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q2. Which chronology card should be filed under What this owner holds and why its previous-year record is stated as zero?

A. The National Commission for Backward Classes page on its own official website, read on 2 September 2026, records that the commission was initially constituted by the central government under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993 dated 2 April 1993 and had been reconstituted seven times up to 2016; that the Act of 1993 was repealed through the National Commission for Backward Classes (Repeal) Act, 2018 dated 14 August 2018; and that the present eighth commission was accorded constitutional status and constituted through the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 dated 11 August 2018, whereby Article 338B was inserted, the commission consisting of a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and three other Members in the rank and pay of Secretary to the Government of India, whose conditions of service and tenure were notified by a Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment notification dated 23 August 2018; nothing beyond those recorded statements is claimed, and no case, complaint, recommendation or list-revision count is asserted.

### Q9. Which statement correctly identifies The Central List route under Article 342A?

A. For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
B. The National Commission for Backward Classes page on its own official website, read on 2 September 2026, records that the commission was initially constituted by the central government under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993 dated 2 April 1993 and had been reconstituted seven times up to 2016; that the Act of 1993 was repealed through the National Commission for Backward Classes (Repeal) Act, 2018 dated 14 August 2018; and that the present eighth commission was accorded constitutional status and constituted through the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 dated 11 August 2018, whereby Article 338B was inserted, the commission consisting of a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and three other Members in the rank and pay of Secretary to the Government of India, whose conditions of service and tenure were notified by a Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment notification dated 23 August 2018; nothing beyond those recorded statements is claimed, and no case, complaint, recommendation or list-revision count is asserted.
C. State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.
D. The owners define the creamy-layer exclusion as a rule resting primarily on the notified parental service and status categories, with an income and wealth test applying where relevant at the notified ceiling of eight lakh rupees measured over three consecutive years, and they add the exclusion that salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; the reason the composition of the rule matters is that a candidate who compresses it into the single sentence that a family income above ₹8 lakh disqualifies an applicant has stated neither the parental status limb nor the income exclusions, and will therefore misread every close option built on the difference between a status ground and an income ground.

### Q29. Which statement correctly identifies What Indra Sawhney decided in 1992?

A. Indra Sawhney against Union of India, decided in 1992, upheld reservation of twenty-seven per cent for the other backward classes in central services and central educational institutions, mandated the creamy-layer exclusion within that category and laid down the ordinary ceiling of fifty per cent on total reservation, and the owners route the detailed constitutional doctrine to the Polity special-provisions owner rather than re-teaching it here; the analytical use of the judgment in a social-justice answer is that it fixed the two features which every later argument turns on, namely a quota expressed as a percentage and an internal equity filter, so any proposal about distribution has to be tested against both.
B. The Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserted Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) and enabled reservation of up to ten per cent in education and public employment for economically weaker citizens who do not fall within the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes or the other backward classes, and the owners insist on the exclusion as part of the definition rather than as a side condition; the consequence for an answer is that the category is defined negatively as well as economically, so an economically poor member of a reserved category is outside it by design, and the criticism that economic backwardness inside the reserved categories is ignored is a criticism of that deliberate design and not evidence of an administrative failure.

**Explanation:** The owners flag the single most productive close-option trap in this topic: the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling for the other backward classes stands at eight lakh rupees and has done so since a Department of Personnel and Training notification of September 2017, and the economically weaker sections income ceiling also stands at eight lakh rupees since January 2019, yet the two are legally different tests, because the first sits on top of notified parental service and status categories and carries income exclusions for salary and agricultural land, while the second is a gross annual family income test paired with notified asset limits; identical arithmetic therefore proves nothing about identical eligibility, and an answer must say which test it is applying. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q49. Which statement correctly identifies The umbrella education-support framework for backward-class students?

A. The owners record the umbrella support framework of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students belonging to the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes, known as PM-YASASVI, whose notified routes include pre-matric and post-matric support and a top-class education route, and they attach two disciplines: the applicable annual guideline decides which component is actually open in which State and year, so no component may be assumed to be universally available, and the economically backward classes served by this framework are not interchangeable with the constitutional economically weaker sections category, because one is a scheme classification and the other is a constitutional route created by amendment.
B. The owners carry the status of the enumeration decision with unusual care as at 21 July 2026: the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 that caste enumeration will be included, the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise, and the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026; the owners then state the boundary, which is that this is an approved and ongoing operation and not published caste data, not a completed methodology and not an automatic basis for revising any list or validating any sub-categorisation formula, so an answer may use the decision as a forthcoming evidence source and must not use it as evidence.
C. Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds.
D. The owners record that the last published all-caste census tables are from 1931 and that the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011 collected caste data which was never officially released or validated for general policy use, and they draw the consequence explicitly: without a published population dataset it is not possible to say whether reservation proportions match population proportions, which sub-groups within the category are actually benefiting, or how outcomes move across cohorts; the discipline for an answer is that this is an evidence gap and not a claim about the size of any community, so no share, count or proportion for the other backward classes may be asserted from it.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** The owners record the umbrella support framework of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students belonging to the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes, known as PM-YASASVI, whose notified routes include pre-matric and post-matric support and a top-class education route, and they attach two disciplines: the applicable annual guideline decides which component is actually open in which State and year, so no component may be assumed to be universally available, and the economically backward classes served by this framework are not interchangeable with the constitutional economically weaker sections category, because one is a scheme classification and the other is a constitutional route created by amendment. The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.

**Answer: B.**
**Explanation:** The owners record that the last published all-caste census tables are from 1931 and that the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011 collected caste data which was never officially released or validated for general policy use, and they draw the consequence explicitly: without a published population dataset it is not possible to say whether reservation proportions match population proportions, which sub-groups within the category are actually benefiting, or how outcomes move across cohorts; the discipline for an answer is that this is an evidence gap and not a claim about the size of any community, so no share, count or proportion for the other backward classes may be asserted from it. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.
B. For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
C. The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.
D. The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it.

**Answer: D.**
**Explanation:** The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q69. Which statement correctly identifies Coverage, first-generation access and intergenerational mobility?

A. The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.
B. The owners state the analytical core of the topic as an identification paradox: reservation for the other backward classes is premised on social and educational backwardness, yet the absence of a published population dataset since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions, evidence-based sub-categorisation and any cohort-level tracking of mobility, so the policy operates without the feedback loop that would tell it whether it is working; the owners are careful that this is a claim about the evidence base and not a claim that the policy has failed, and an answer should use it to argue for measurement design rather than to assert an outcome that the data cannot support.
C. The audited routing ledgers, which cover the Mains papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025, route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner, and both the Basic and the Advanced owner state independently that no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question on this subject matter exists in the audited local texts, which is consistent with that zero; the 2022 General Studies Paper II demand on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status is routed to the Polity commissions owner and the 2018 General Studies Paper II demand on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions is routed there as well, so neither is claimed here, and the practice in this package is therefore six original Mains questions with model solutions written to the repository's examiner-grade standard together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A → B → C → D rotation.
D. This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.

### Q72. Which statement avoids a close-option trap about Coverage, first-generation access and intergenerational mobility?

A. State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.
B. For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.

### Q78. Which chronology card should be filed under The transparent ownership statement for this owner?
