## PYQS AND ANSWER PRACTICE

### TRANSPARENT ZERO-DIRECT-PYQ AUDIT

The audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner for the covered years, and that zero is reported openly rather than concealed or filled with a manufactured demand card. The four audited ledgers consulted are the Mains routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims routing ledgers for 2018 to 2023 and for 2024 to 2025, and neither the Basic owner nor the Advanced owner for this topic carries a generated previous-year integration block, which is itself consistent with that zero. Both owners state independently that there is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question specifically on the other backward classes or the economically weaker sections in the audited local previous-year texts, and this package adopts exactly that position. The two nearest neighbouring demands are the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from a statutory to a constitutional body, and the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger routes both to the Polity commissions owner together with the consolidated body-design owner, so no ownership is claimed over either here; reservation doctrine such as the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling and the constitutional validity reasoning for the economic criterion is likewise routed to the Polity special-provisions owner and is cross-linked rather than duplicated. The practice in this package therefore consists of six original Mains questions with model solutions, each written to the repository's examiner-grade standard using the claim, named evidence, analysis and qualification pattern, together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A → B → C → D rotation. Each model solution opens with its analytical claim so that the evidence which follows does argumentative rather than decorative work. The locally held OCR-searchable official General Studies papers were read only to confirm that no question on this owner's subject matter is routed here; no question was invented from them, no marking scheme or page precision was imported and no official answer key is claimed for any question anywhere in this package.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “TRANSPARENT ZERO-DIRECT-PYQ AUDIT”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** The answer must resolve the Social Justice demand in “TRANSPARENT ZERO-DIRECT-PYQ AUDIT”.

**Analytical body:**

1. **Claim and named evidence:** Define the vulnerable group, deprivation, right or policy concept and distinguish the nearest legal and measured category. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
2. **Claim and named evidence:** Identify the constitutional, statutory, judicial or executive basis and the competent Union, state or local institution. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
3. **Claim and named evidence:** Map rights-holder, household/community context, duty-bearer, frontline worker, provider and accountability forum. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
4. **Claim and named evidence:** Trace identification, eligibility, documentation, finance, access, portability, grievance, appeal and correction. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
5. **Claim and named evidence:** Use a named Indian law, judgment, scheme, institution or official dataset with source, date, denominator and status. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
6. **Claim and named evidence:** Test intersectionality, regional variation, stigma, accessibility, privacy, fiscal adequacy, exclusion and causal limits. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

**Counter-position / limit:** A constitutional promise, prohibition, reservation provision, scheme catalogue, allocation, registration count or headline statistic cannot alone establish accessible implementation, adequate benefit, dignity, remedy or attributable outcome; test eligibility, institutions, frontline capacity, intersectionality, regional variation, grievance and evidence status.

**Qualified conclusion:** The answer must resolve the Social Justice demand in “TRANSPARENT ZERO-DIRECT-PYQ AUDIT”.

**Executable exam-length answer / compression plan:** For 15 marks, spend one-sixth of the time decoding the directive and drawing right/need → institution → eligibility → delivery → outcome → remedy; state a thesis; write four to seven claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification points; reserve the final minute for dignity, accessibility, portability, grievance, denominator, fiscal and causal limits.

**Why this earns marks:** The answer obeys the directive, explains mechanisms rather than listing schemes, uses named India-centric evidence and preserves legal, institutional, group, eligibility, implementation, fiscal, data and outcome distinctions.

**How to improve this answer:** For “TRANSPARENT ZERO-DIRECT-PYQ AUDIT”, replace the weakest scheme-catalogue point with one named rights-holder, duty-bearer, eligibility bottleneck, safeguard, grievance route, process/outcome indicator and source-status qualification.

#### 10. PYQ-based analytical application

- ⚠️ There is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 GS-II Mains PYQ specifically on OBC/EWS
  in the audited local PYQ texts. A strong Mains answer should: (a) distinguish
  social-educational backwardness (OBC) from economic backwardness (EWS), (b) explain
  the creamy-layer rationale and its limitations, (c) cite Janhit Abhiyan (2022) for
  EWS constitutional validity, (d) note the caste-data gap and the 2027 Census decision,
  and (e) frame reservation as a means to social mobility, not an end in itself.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 1 — 10 MARKS

**Question:** Distinguish reservation for the other backward classes from reservation for the economically weaker sections with reference to their constitutional basis and identification criteria. Answer in about 150 words.

**Model thesis:** The two routes rest on different enabling provisions and different tests, so the distinction must name Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) against Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), then show that social and educational backwardness and a notified income-and-asset test are not interchangeable evidence.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.
- The Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserted Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) and enabled reservation of up to ten per cent in education and public employment for economically weaker citizens who do not fall within the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes or the other backward classes, and the owners insist on the exclusion as part of the definition rather than as a side condition; the consequence for an answer is that the category is defined negatively as well as economically, so an economically poor member of a reserved category is outside it by design, and the criticism that economic backwardness inside the reserved categories is ignored is a criticism of that deliberate design and not evidence of an administrative failure.
- The owners record that the criteria were defined by a Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019, under which the gross annual family income must be below eight lakh rupees and the family must not own five acres or more of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more, a residential plot of 100 sq yards or more in a notified municipality, or 200 sq yards or more elsewhere; the owners add that these figures have remained unchanged since 2019 and were before the Supreme Court when the amendment was upheld in November 2022, and the discipline is to quote the asset limits together with the income limit, because an answer that cites only the income figure has described half of a two-limbed test.
- The owners flag the single most productive close-option trap in this topic: the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling for the other backward classes stands at eight lakh rupees and has done so since a Department of Personnel and Training notification of September 2017, and the economically weaker sections income ceiling also stands at eight lakh rupees since January 2019, yet the two are legally different tests, because the first sits on top of notified parental service and status categories and carries income exclusions for salary and agricultural land, while the second is a gross annual family income test paired with notified asset limits; identical arithmetic therefore proves nothing about identical eligibility, and an answer must say which test it is applying.

**Qualified conclusion:** The two routes rest on different enabling provisions and different tests, so the distinction must name Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) against Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), then show that social and educational backwardness and a notified income-and-asset test are not interchangeable evidence.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “Distinguish reservation for the other backward classes from reservation for the economically…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** The two routes rest on different enabling provisions and different tests, so the distinction must name Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) against Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), then show that social and educational backwardness and a notified income-and-asset test are not interchangeable evidence.

**Analytical body:**

1. **Claim and named evidence:** The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 2 — 10 MARKS

**Question:** Explain the rationale of the creamy-layer exclusion within backward-class reservation and examine its limitations. Answer in about 150 words.

**Model thesis:** The exclusion exists to stop repeated capture by the already advanced inside a listed category, so the explanation must state its status limb before its income limb and then locate its limits in threshold lag and in the absence of any comparable filter elsewhere.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The owners define the creamy-layer exclusion as a rule resting primarily on the notified parental service and status categories, with an income and wealth test applying where relevant at the notified ceiling of eight lakh rupees measured over three consecutive years, and they add the exclusion that salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; the reason the composition of the rule matters is that a candidate who compresses it into the single sentence that a family income above ₹8 lakh disqualifies an applicant has stated neither the parental status limb nor the income exclusions, and will therefore misread every close option built on the difference between a status ground and an income ground.
- The owners state without qualification that the creamy-layer exclusion applies to reservation for the other backward classes and does not apply to reservation for the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes, and they attribute that position to the decision in Indra Sawhney against Union of India and the jurisprudence that followed it; the trap this fact defuses is the very common transfer of an other-backward-class filter to a Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe answer, and the corresponding trap in the opposite direction, which is to assume that because a category is listed it must carry the same internal filter as every other listed category.
- Indra Sawhney against Union of India, decided in 1992, upheld reservation of twenty-seven per cent for the other backward classes in central services and central educational institutions, mandated the creamy-layer exclusion within that category and laid down the ordinary ceiling of fifty per cent on total reservation, and the owners route the detailed constitutional doctrine to the Polity special-provisions owner rather than re-teaching it here; the analytical use of the judgment in a social-justice answer is that it fixed the two features which every later argument turns on, namely a quota expressed as a percentage and an internal equity filter, so any proposal about distribution has to be tested against both.
- The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.

**Qualified conclusion:** The exclusion exists to stop repeated capture by the already advanced inside a listed category, so the explanation must state its status limb before its income limb and then locate its limits in threshold lag and in the absence of any comparable filter elsewhere.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **explain** requires a direct position on “Explain the rationale of the creamy-layer exclusion within backward-class reservation and…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** The exclusion exists to stop repeated capture by the already advanced inside a listed category, so the explanation must state its status limb before its income limb and then locate its limits in threshold lag and in the absence of any comparable filter elsewhere.

**Analytical body:**

1. **Claim and named evidence:** The owners define the creamy-layer exclusion as a rule resting primarily on the notified parental service and status categories, with an income and wealth test applying where relevant at the notified ceiling of eight lakh rupees measured over three consecutive years, and they add the exclusion that salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; the reason the composition of the rule matters is that a candidate who compresses it into the single sentence that a family income above ₹8 lakh disqualifies an applicant has stated neither the parental status limb nor the income exclusions, and will therefore misread every close option built on the difference between a status ground and an income ground. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 3 — 15 MARKS

**Question:** Critically examine the claim that the absence of published caste data undermines evidence-based backward-class policy. Answer in about 250 words.

**Model thesis:** The gap bites on calibration rather than on entitlement, so the examination must separate what cannot be computed without a dataset from what remains constitutionally secure with one, and must treat the forthcoming enumeration as a future source and not as present evidence.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The owners record that the last published all-caste census tables are from 1931 and that the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011 collected caste data which was never officially released or validated for general policy use, and they draw the consequence explicitly: without a published population dataset it is not possible to say whether reservation proportions match population proportions, which sub-groups within the category are actually benefiting, or how outcomes move across cohorts; the discipline for an answer is that this is an evidence gap and not a claim about the size of any community, so no share, count or proportion for the other backward classes may be asserted from it.
- The owners carry the status of the enumeration decision with unusual care as at 21 July 2026: the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 that caste enumeration will be included, the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise, and the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026; the owners then state the boundary, which is that this is an approved and ongoing operation and not published caste data, not a completed methodology and not an automatic basis for revising any list or validating any sub-categorisation formula, so an answer may use the decision as a forthcoming evidence source and must not use it as evidence.
- The owners state the analytical core of the topic as an identification paradox: reservation for the other backward classes is premised on social and educational backwardness, yet the absence of a published population dataset since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions, evidence-based sub-categorisation and any cohort-level tracking of mobility, so the policy operates without the feedback loop that would tell it whether it is working; the owners are careful that this is a claim about the evidence base and not a claim that the policy has failed, and an answer should use it to argue for measurement design rather than to assert an outcome that the data cannot support.
- Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds.

**Qualified conclusion:** The gap bites on calibration rather than on entitlement, so the examination must separate what cannot be computed without a dataset from what remains constitutionally secure with one, and must treat the forthcoming enumeration as a future source and not as present evidence.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **critically examine** requires a direct position on “Critically examine the claim that the absence of published caste data undermines evidence-…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 4 — 15 MARKS

**Question:** Assess the constitutional reasoning by which reservation on a purely economic criterion was upheld in 2022 and the objections it left open. Answer in about 250 words.

**Model thesis:** A three-to-two majority is an argument and not a consensus, so the assessment must state the majority reasoning on a new category and the ceiling, state the dissent on substantive equality, and tie both to the exact notified test that was before the Court.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserted Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) and enabled reservation of up to ten per cent in education and public employment for economically weaker citizens who do not fall within the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes or the other backward classes, and the owners insist on the exclusion as part of the definition rather than as a side condition; the consequence for an answer is that the category is defined negatively as well as economically, so an economically poor member of a reserved category is outside it by design, and the criticism that economic backwardness inside the reserved categories is ignored is a criticism of that deliberate design and not evidence of an administrative failure.
- Janhit Abhiyan against Union of India, decided in November 2022 by a majority of three to two, upheld the economically weaker sections reservation, the majority reasoning that an economic criterion alone can be a basis for affirmative action and that going beyond the fifty per cent ceiling does not violate the basic structure when the addition creates a new category rather than enlarging the existing reserved categories, while the dissent argued that an economic criterion cannot be used to exclude the existing reserved categories from a new benefit without violating substantive equality; the owners require both limbs to be stated, because reciting only the outcome converts a closely divided constitutional argument into a settled consensus.
- The owners record that the criteria were defined by a Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019, under which the gross annual family income must be below eight lakh rupees and the family must not own five acres or more of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more, a residential plot of 100 sq yards or more in a notified municipality, or 200 sq yards or more elsewhere; the owners add that these figures have remained unchanged since 2019 and were before the Supreme Court when the amendment was upheld in November 2022, and the discipline is to quote the asset limits together with the income limit, because an answer that cites only the income figure has described half of a two-limbed test.
- The owners flag the single most productive close-option trap in this topic: the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling for the other backward classes stands at eight lakh rupees and has done so since a Department of Personnel and Training notification of September 2017, and the economically weaker sections income ceiling also stands at eight lakh rupees since January 2019, yet the two are legally different tests, because the first sits on top of notified parental service and status categories and carries income exclusions for salary and agricultural land, while the second is a gross annual family income test paired with notified asset limits; identical arithmetic therefore proves nothing about identical eligibility, and an answer must say which test it is applying.

**Qualified conclusion:** A three-to-two majority is an argument and not a consensus, so the assessment must state the majority reasoning on a new category and the ceiling, state the dissent on substantive equality, and tie both to the exact notified test that was before the Court.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **assess** requires a direct position on “Assess the constitutional reasoning by which reservation on a purely economic criterion was…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 5 — 20 MARKS

**Question:** Analyse why reservation and scholarship coverage is an inadequate measure of social mobility, and what a better measurement architecture would require. Answer in about 300 words.

**Model thesis:** Coverage counts an administrative event while mobility is a cohort outcome, so the analysis must separate coverage, first-generation access and intergenerational progression, then specify the data, the unit and the time horizon that a mobility measure actually needs.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.
- The owners record the umbrella support framework of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students belonging to the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes, known as PM-YASASVI, whose notified routes include pre-matric and post-matric support and a top-class education route, and they attach two disciplines: the applicable annual guideline decides which component is actually open in which State and year, so no component may be assumed to be universally available, and the economically backward classes served by this framework are not interchangeable with the constitutional economically weaker sections category, because one is a scheme classification and the other is a constitutional route created by amendment.
- The owners state the analytical core of the topic as an identification paradox: reservation for the other backward classes is premised on social and educational backwardness, yet the absence of a published population dataset since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions, evidence-based sub-categorisation and any cohort-level tracking of mobility, so the policy operates without the feedback loop that would tell it whether it is working; the owners are careful that this is a claim about the evidence base and not a claim that the policy has failed, and an answer should use it to argue for measurement design rather than to assert an outcome that the data cannot support.
- The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it.

**Qualified conclusion:** Coverage counts an administrative event while mobility is a cohort outcome, so the analysis must separate coverage, first-generation access and intergenerational progression, then specify the data, the unit and the time horizon that a mobility measure actually needs.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **analyse** requires a direct position on “Analyse why reservation and scholarship coverage is an inadequate measure of social mobility,…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.
