### ORIGINAL MAINS 6 — 20 MARKS

**Question:** Evaluate the proposition that backward-class policy in India has solved identification but not distribution. Answer in about 300 words.

**Model thesis:** Identification and distribution are different problems with different instruments, so the evaluation must show that the list-making route is settled while the division of the quota inside the category is not, and must close with a graded verdict that is honest about the missing evidence.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
- State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.
- Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds.
- The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.

**Qualified conclusion:** Identification and distribution are different problems with different instruments, so the evaluation must show that the list-making route is settled while the division of the quota inside the category is not, and must close with a graded verdict that is honest about the missing evidence.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **evaluate** requires a direct position on “Evaluate the proposition that backward-class policy in India has solved identification but…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** Identification and distribution are different problems with different instruments, so the evaluation must show that the list-making route is settled while the division of the quota inside the category is not, and must close with a graded verdict that is honest about the missing evidence.

**Analytical body:**

1. **Claim and named evidence:** For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
2. **Claim and named evidence:** State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
3. **Claim and named evidence:** Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
4. **Claim and named evidence:** The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

**Counter-position / limit:** A constitutional promise, prohibition, reservation provision, scheme catalogue, allocation, registration count or headline statistic cannot alone establish accessible implementation, adequate benefit, dignity, remedy or attributable outcome; test eligibility, institutions, frontline capacity, intersectionality, regional variation, grievance and evidence status.

**Qualified conclusion:** Identification and distribution are different problems with different instruments, so the evaluation must show that the list-making route is settled while the division of the quota inside the category is not, and must close with a graded verdict that is honest about the missing evidence.

**Executable exam-length answer / compression plan:** For 20 marks, spend one-sixth of the time decoding the directive and drawing right/need → institution → eligibility → delivery → outcome → remedy; state a thesis; write four to seven claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification points; reserve the final minute for dignity, accessibility, portability, grievance, denominator, fiscal and causal limits.

**Why this earns marks:** The answer obeys the directive, explains mechanisms rather than listing schemes, uses named India-centric evidence and preserves legal, institutional, group, eligibility, implementation, fiscal, data and outcome distinctions.

**How to improve this answer:** For “Evaluate the proposition that backward-class policy in India has solved identification but…”, replace the weakest scheme-catalogue point with one named rights-holder, duty-bearer, eligibility bottleneck, safeguard, grievance route, process/outcome indicator and source-status qualification.

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

> **Subject:** Social Justice | **Tier:** Advanced | **GS Paper:** GS-II;
> cross-cuts GS-I (social stratification, mobility) and GS-III (inclusive development).
> **Core area:** Other Backward Classes, Economically Weaker Sections, creamy layer,
> sub-categorisation, social mobility measurement, caste-data gap.
> **Grounded in:** Indra Sawhney v. Union of India (1992); Janhit Abhiyan v. Union of
> India (2022); 102nd, 103rd, 105th Constitutional Amendment Acts; Rohini Commission
> (2017); PM-YASASVI; SECC 2011 (unreleased OBC data); Census Act, 1948.
> ✅ = source-grounded | ⚠️ = inference/analysis | 📰 = current anchor.
> *Companion: `basic/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md`.*

#### 1. Architecture

```text
IDENTIFICATION LAYER                    BENEFIT LAYER
       |                                      |
  Central List <--- NCBC advice              OBC 27%
  (President /      (constitutional)         reservation
   Parliament varies)
       |                                      |
  State Lists  <--- State govts              EWS 10%
  (105th Amdt       (restored power)         reservation
   2021)                                      |
       |                                 Creamy layer
       v                                 filter (OBC)
  CASTE-DATA GAP                              |
  (no OBC count       ------------------>  PM-YASASVI
   since 1931)                            scholarships
       |                                      |
  Census 2027                                 v
  operations begun --------------------> MOBILITY OUTCOME
  (no results yet)                       (inter-generational
                                          education/occupation)
```

**Analytical claim:** ⚠️ OBC policy operates with an identification paradox: reservation
is premised on social-educational backwardness, but the absence of OBC population data
since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions,
sub-categorisation and mobility tracking. The 2027 Census caste enumeration may resolve
this paradox, but its policy operationalisation is not yet settled.

#### 2. Concepts and distinctions

| Concept | Precise meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ **Social-educational backwardness vs economic backwardness** | OBC identification is based on social and educational backwardness (caste/community-based historical disadvantage), not solely income; EWS identification is based purely on economic criteria (income and asset thresholds) for non-SC/ST/OBC citizens — the two are conceptually and constitutionally distinct. |
| ✅ **Creamy layer (status + income/wealth test)** | The exclusion rule combines notified parental service/status categories with an income/wealth test where applicable. The ₹8 lakh threshold applies over three consecutive years; salary income and agricultural-land income are not simply clubbed into it. |
| ✅ **Sub-categorisation within OBCs** | The proposal (examined by the Rohini Commission, 2017) to divide the 27% OBC quota among sub-groups to ensure equitable distribution and prevent concentration of benefits among a few dominant OBC communities. |
| ⚠️ **Intergenerational mobility vs first-generation access** | Reservation provides first-generation access (entry to education/employment); true mobility is measured by whether subsequent generations consolidate and advance — a distinction often conflated in policy discourse. |
| ⚠️ **Caste-data gap** | The last published all-caste census tables are from 1931; SECC 2011 collected caste data but it was not released/validated for general policy use. Caste enumeration is approved for Census 2027, but as at 21 July 2026 it has produced no results. |

#### 3. Detailed causal chain: identification to mobility

1. **Community identification:** A community's social-educational backwardness is
   assessed (traditionally by state backward-class commissions, now NCBC for central
   list) based on indicators such as caste-based occupation, literacy, representation
   in services, and social stigma.
2. **List inclusion:** Article 342A governs the Central List (President specifies;
   Parliament varies), with NCBC advice/consultation in the institutional process. States
   maintain separate lists for State purposes after the 105th Amendment.
3. **Creamy-layer filter:** An individual from a listed OBC community must obtain a non-
   creamy-layer certificate to claim reservation benefits, ensuring the economically/
   socially advanced within the category do not capture benefits meant for the backward.
4. **Reservation uptake:** The individual accesses reserved seats in education or
   employment, gaining first-generation entry into higher education or government service.
5. **Welfare schemes:** PM-YASASVI provides notified education-support routes for
   OBC/EBC/DNT students, complementing reservation. State/year-specific components must
   be checked before claiming a particular scholarship is open.
6. **First-generation to second-generation:** If the beneficiary's children achieve
   higher educational attainment and occupational status, intergenerational mobility is
   realised; if they merely re-access reservation without progression, mobility stagnates.
7. **Policy feedback (currently constrained):** Without published OBC population data,
   cannot assess: (a) whether reservation proportions match population proportions,
   (b) which sub-groups within OBCs are benefiting, (c) mobility outcomes over cohorts.

#### 4. Institutional and reform architecture

- ✅ **NCBC (constitutional body, post-2018):** Examines inclusion/exclusion requests,
  advises on backwardness criteria, monitors safeguards and handles complaints. It does
  not itself amend the Article 342A Central List.
- ✅ **Rohini Commission (2017):** Set up to examine sub-categorisation of OBCs within
  the 27% quota — the commission's report would enable equitable distribution among
  OBC sub-groups, but implementation remains pending as of mid-2026.
- ✅ **EWS income/asset criteria:** Defined by a DoPT notification dated 31 January
  2019: gross annual family income below ₹8 lakh, and family must not own 5 acres
  or more of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more, a
  residential plot of 100 sq yards or more in a notified municipality, or 200 sq
  yards or more elsewhere. These figures have remained unchanged since 2019 and
  were upheld by the Supreme Court in November 2022 (Janhit Abhiyan).
- ⚠️ **EWS certificate verification challenge:** Unlike caste certificates (linked to
  hereditary community identity), EWS certificates require income verification — a
  harder administrative task, leading to concerns about fake certificates and exclusion
  of genuinely poor applicants lacking documentation.

#### 5. Indian applications and boundary cases

- ⚠️ A dominant OBC community in a state may capture a disproportionate share of OBC
  reservation seats, leaving numerically smaller or more backward OBC communities
  under-represented — the rationale for sub-categorisation.
- ⚠️ An EWS applicant with undocumented informal-sector income may fail to obtain a
  certificate despite genuine economic weakness, or conversely, an applicant with
  concealed income may fraudulently obtain one — the identification gap.
- ⚠️ The Janhit Abhiyan majority (3-2) upheld EWS reservation on the ground that economic
  criterion alone can be a basis for affirmative action and that exceeding 50% ceiling
  does not violate basic structure when the addition is for a new category (not within
  existing SC/ST/OBC); the dissent argued economic criterion cannot exclude existing
  reserved categories from a new benefit without violating substantive equality.

#### 6. Limitations and trade-offs

- ⚠️ **Creamy-layer threshold lag:** The income ceiling is revised periodically but often
  lags behind inflation, leading to either over-inclusion (ceiling too high) or under-
  inclusion (ceiling too low) of genuinely backward OBC applicants.
- ⚠️ **EWS excludes SC/ST/OBC:** By definition, EWS reservation is for non-SC/ST/OBC
  citizens; an economically poor SC/ST/OBC individual is not eligible for EWS but may
  already have access to their respective reservation — the design is intentional but
  generates criticism that economic backwardness within reserved categories is ignored.
- ⚠️ **Data blindness:** Without published OBC population data, sub-categorisation is
  politically contested and technically difficult. Census 2027 operations have begun,
  but its results, validation and policy use are not yet available.
- ⚠️ **Mobility vs coverage:** Policy discourse focuses on reservation-slot fill rates
  and scholarship disbursement, not on whether beneficiaries achieve intergenerational
  mobility — a measurement gap.

#### 7. Must-Know Facts for Advanced Prelims

- ✅ Indra Sawhney v. Union of India (1992) upheld OBC reservation up to 27% and mandated
  creamy-layer exclusion for OBCs (not SC/ST).
- ✅ The 102nd Constitutional Amendment (2018) made NCBC a constitutional body but was
  interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case as removing states'
  power to prepare OBC lists — the 105th Amendment (2021) corrected this.
- ✅ The 103rd Constitutional Amendment (2019) enabled EWS reservation; Janhit Abhiyan
  v. Union of India (November 2022) upheld it by a 3-2 majority.
- ✅ The SECC 2011 collected caste data, but the OBC data was never officially released
  or validated for policy use.
- ✅ The OBC income/wealth ceiling (₹8 lakh, since September 2017) and EWS income
  ceiling (₹8 lakh, since January 2019) are numerically identical but legally different:
  the former includes parental service/status categories and income exclusions; the
  latter also has notified asset limits.

#### 8. Advanced Prelims traps

- ❌ The 102nd Amendment gave states the power to prepare their own OBC lists. -> ✅ The
  102nd Amendment was interpreted as centralising list-making power with Parliament;
  the 105th Amendment (2021) explicitly restored states' power for state-level lists.
- ❌ Sub-categorisation of OBCs has been implemented nationally. -> ✅ Sub-categorisation
  is under examination (Rohini Commission, 2017); implementation remains pending as of
  mid-2026.
- ❌ EWS reservation is available to economically poor OBCs. -> ✅ EWS reservation under
  the 103rd Amendment explicitly excludes SC/ST/OBC; it is for non-reserved category
  citizens meeting economic criteria only.

#### 9. 📰 Current-anchor note

- 📰 **Census 2027 status (21 July 2026):** CCPA's 30 April 2025 decision includes caste
  enumeration. First-phase houselisting is notified for 1 April–30 September 2026 in an
  all-digital Census. It is not a published dataset and cannot yet validate a
  sub-categorisation formula or revise a list.
- ✅ The OBC income/wealth ceiling is ₹8 lakh (September 2017), but it is not the same
  test as the EWS ₹8 lakh-plus-asset criterion. State the parental status and income
  exclusions before drawing a comparison.

#### 10. PYQ-based analytical application

- ⚠️ There is no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 GS-II Mains PYQ specifically on OBC/EWS
  in the audited local PYQ texts. A strong Mains answer should: (a) distinguish
  social-educational backwardness (OBC) from economic backwardness (EWS), (b) explain
  the creamy-layer rationale and its limitations, (c) cite Janhit Abhiyan (2022) for
  EWS constitutional validity, (d) note the caste-data gap and the 2027 Census decision,
  and (e) frame reservation as a means to social mobility, not an end in itself.

#### 11. Mains-ready framework

**Central thesis:** OBC reservation addresses caste-based social-educational backwardness
with a creamy-layer filter to ensure intra-group equity, while EWS reservation addresses
pure economic disadvantage among non-reserved categories; both suffer from an
identification-to-outcome gap — OBC policy lacks population data for calibration, EWS
policy faces income-verification challenges — and their success must be measured by
intergenerational social mobility, not coverage statistics alone.

1. **Define the category and its constitutional basis:** OBC under Art 15(4)/16(4), EWS
   under Art 15(6)/16(6) (103rd Amendment, 2019).
2. **Explain the identification mechanism:** Article 342A's President/Parliament route
   for the Central List; state governments for State lists (105th Amendment); income/
   asset verification for an EWS certificate.
3. **Distinguish the filter:** creamy layer for OBC (parental status plus applicable
   income/wealth test) versus the EWS income/asset threshold — different mechanisms,
   different rationales.
4. **Flag the data gap:** no published OBC population dataset; SECC 2011 data unreleased;
   Census 2027 enumeration approved and operations begun, but no results or validated
   policy dataset exists.
5. **Link to outcome:** Reservation provides first-generation access; social mobility is
   measured by second-generation progression — policy should track mobility, not merely
   coverage.
6. **Recommend:** Sub-categorisation (for intra-OBC equity), robust EWS verification,
   mobility tracking using 2027 Census data once available.

#### 12. Probable questions

- ⚠️ **Prelims:** Which commission was set up in 2017 to examine sub-categorisation of
  OBCs within the 27% reservation quota?
- ⚠️ **Mains (10 marks):** Explain the rationale for the creamy-layer exclusion in OBC
  reservation and discuss its limitations.
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** "The caste-data gap undermines evidence-based backward-class
  policy." Critically analyse this statement in light of the 2027 Census caste-
  enumeration decision.
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** Distinguish OBC reservation from EWS reservation with reference
  to their constitutional basis, identification criteria, and judicial pronouncements.
  How do they together advance social justice?

#### 13. Study links

- ✅ Foundation companion: `basic/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md`.
- ✅ `Polity/advanced/22_Special-Provisions.md` — reservation doctrine, 103rd Amendment
  validity (Janhit Abhiyan), 50% ceiling jurisprudence.
- ✅ `Polity/advanced/31_National-Commissions-SC-ST-BC.md` — NCBC constitutional status,
  composition and mandate.
- ✅ `basic/07_Scheduled-Castes-Rights-Atrocities-and-Welfare.md` — SC reservation and
  welfare architecture, for comparison with OBC/EWS targeting.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

### OBC, EWS and Social Mobility: RAPID RIGHT, SCHEME AND MEASURED-OUTCOME MAP

1. **What this owner holds and why its previous-year record is stated as zero:** This owner holds the backward-class half of the affirmative-action argument: the identification of socially and educationally backward classes for the Central and State lists, the creamy-layer filter that runs inside the other backward classes, the separate economically weaker sections category created in 2019, the education-support framework for backward-class students, the sub-categorisation debate and the caste-data gap that constrains all of them; its distinctive feature is that the audited routing ledgers route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to it for the covered years, so the zero is stated openly here, and the two neighbouring commission demands, the 2018 General Studies Paper II question on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions and the 2022 General Studies Paper II question on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status, are both routed by the audited 2018-2023 Mains ledger to the Polity commissions owner and are therefore not claimed here.
2. **The constitutional and statutory bank for a backward-class answer:** The owners record the law bank precisely: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) permit special provision and reservation for socially and educationally backward classes, Article 342A governs the Central List of socially and educationally backward classes, Article 338B carries the national commission for those classes after the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018, and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6), inserted by the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019, enable reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections; the statutory layer is the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, which was repealed once the commission was given constitutional status, and the answer-writing discipline is that the backward-class route and the economic-criterion route are two constitutionally distinct routes and must never be merged into one reservation story.
3. **The Central List route under Article 342A:** For central purposes the Constitution routes list-making through Article 342A, under which the President specifies the socially and educationally backward classes and Parliament alone may include a community in or exclude a community from that list, while the national commission examines inclusion and exclusion requests, advises on the criteria of backwardness, monitors safeguards and handles complaints; the examinable consequence is that the commission is a recommending and advisory institution inside the list-making process rather than an authority that can itself add a community, so a candidate who writes that the commission added a caste to the Central List has confused advice with legislation.
4. **State lists and the restoration effected by the 105th Amendment:** State governments maintain their own lists of backward classes for State-level reservation, and the owners record the sequence that produced the present position: the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 was interpreted by the Supreme Court in the Maratha reservation case in 2021 as centralising list-making power in Parliament, and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021 then clarified that the President's list binds only central purposes and expressly restored the power of States to prepare their own lists of socially and educationally backward classes for State purposes; the discipline is that the 102nd Amendment did not grant that State power and the 105th Amendment did not create the category, it restored a competence that a judicial reading had removed.
5. **The commission's own account of its constitutional status:** The National Commission for Backward Classes page on its own official website, read on 2 September 2026, records that the commission was initially constituted by the central government under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993 dated 2 April 1993 and had been reconstituted seven times up to 2016; that the Act of 1993 was repealed through the National Commission for Backward Classes (Repeal) Act, 2018 dated 14 August 2018; and that the present eighth commission was accorded constitutional status and constituted through the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 dated 11 August 2018, whereby Article 338B was inserted, the commission consisting of a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and three other Members in the rank and pay of Secretary to the Government of India, whose conditions of service and tenure were notified by a Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment notification dated 23 August 2018; nothing beyond those recorded statements is claimed, and no case, complaint, recommendation or list-revision count is asserted.
6. **The creamy layer is a status test before it is an income test:** The owners define the creamy-layer exclusion as a rule resting primarily on the notified parental service and status categories, with an income and wealth test applying where relevant at the notified ceiling of eight lakh rupees measured over three consecutive years, and they add the exclusion that salary income and income from agricultural land are not simply clubbed into that test; the reason the composition of the rule matters is that a candidate who compresses it into the single sentence that a family income above ₹8 lakh disqualifies an applicant has stated neither the parental status limb nor the income exclusions, and will therefore misread every close option built on the difference between a status ground and an income ground.
7. **Creamy layer is confined to the other backward classes:** The owners state without qualification that the creamy-layer exclusion applies to reservation for the other backward classes and does not apply to reservation for the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes, and they attribute that position to the decision in Indra Sawhney against Union of India and the jurisprudence that followed it; the trap this fact defuses is the very common transfer of an other-backward-class filter to a Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe answer, and the corresponding trap in the opposite direction, which is to assume that because a category is listed it must carry the same internal filter as every other listed category.
8. **What Indra Sawhney decided in 1992:** Indra Sawhney against Union of India, decided in 1992, upheld reservation of twenty-seven per cent for the other backward classes in central services and central educational institutions, mandated the creamy-layer exclusion within that category and laid down the ordinary ceiling of fifty per cent on total reservation, and the owners route the detailed constitutional doctrine to the Polity special-provisions owner rather than re-teaching it here; the analytical use of the judgment in a social-justice answer is that it fixed the two features which every later argument turns on, namely a quota expressed as a percentage and an internal equity filter, so any proposal about distribution has to be tested against both.
9. **The economically weaker sections category and the 103rd Amendment:** The Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserted Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) and enabled reservation of up to ten per cent in education and public employment for economically weaker citizens who do not fall within the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes or the other backward classes, and the owners insist on the exclusion as part of the definition rather than as a side condition; the consequence for an answer is that the category is defined negatively as well as economically, so an economically poor member of a reserved category is outside it by design, and the criticism that economic backwardness inside the reserved categories is ignored is a criticism of that deliberate design and not evidence of an administrative failure.
10. **The notified income and asset criteria for that category:** The owners record that the criteria were defined by a Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019, under which the gross annual family income must be below eight lakh rupees and the family must not own five acres or more of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more, a residential plot of 100 sq yards or more in a notified municipality, or 200 sq yards or more elsewhere; the owners add that these figures have remained unchanged since 2019 and were before the Supreme Court when the amendment was upheld in November 2022, and the discipline is to quote the asset limits together with the income limit, because an answer that cites only the income figure has described half of a two-limbed test.
11. **Janhit Abhiyan in November 2022 and the shape of the disagreement:** Janhit Abhiyan against Union of India, decided in November 2022 by a majority of three to two, upheld the economically weaker sections reservation, the majority reasoning that an economic criterion alone can be a basis for affirmative action and that going beyond the fifty per cent ceiling does not violate the basic structure when the addition creates a new category rather than enlarging the existing reserved categories, while the dissent argued that an economic criterion cannot be used to exclude the existing reserved categories from a new benefit without violating substantive equality; the owners require both limbs to be stated, because reciting only the outcome converts a closely divided constitutional argument into a settled consensus.
12. **Why two identical rupee figures are two different legal tests:** The owners flag the single most productive close-option trap in this topic: the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling for the other backward classes stands at eight lakh rupees and has done so since a Department of Personnel and Training notification of September 2017, and the economically weaker sections income ceiling also stands at eight lakh rupees since January 2019, yet the two are legally different tests, because the first sits on top of notified parental service and status categories and carries income exclusions for salary and agricultural land, while the second is a gross annual family income test paired with notified asset limits; identical arithmetic therefore proves nothing about identical eligibility, and an answer must say which test it is applying.
13. **The umbrella education-support framework for backward-class students:** The owners record the umbrella support framework of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students belonging to the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes, known as PM-YASASVI, whose notified routes include pre-matric and post-matric support and a top-class education route, and they attach two disciplines: the applicable annual guideline decides which component is actually open in which State and year, so no component may be assumed to be universally available, and the economically backward classes served by this framework are not interchangeable with the constitutional economically weaker sections category, because one is a scheme classification and the other is a constitutional route created by amendment.
14. **Sub-categorisation and the commission that examined it:** Sub-categorisation is the proposal to divide the twenty-seven per cent quota for the other backward classes among sub-groups so that the benefit is not concentrated in a few relatively advanced communities, and the owners record that the Rohini Commission was set up in 2017 to examine it and that implementation remained pending as of mid-2026; the owners also record why the question is hard rather than merely political, namely that a defensible division requires backwardness and representation data for each sub-group, so the measure that would correct unequal distribution depends on precisely the evidence that the caste-data gap withholds.
15. **The caste-data gap and what the 2011 socio-economic survey did not settle:** The owners record that the last published all-caste census tables are from 1931 and that the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011 collected caste data which was never officially released or validated for general policy use, and they draw the consequence explicitly: without a published population dataset it is not possible to say whether reservation proportions match population proportions, which sub-groups within the category are actually benefiting, or how outcomes move across cohorts; the discipline for an answer is that this is an evidence gap and not a claim about the size of any community, so no share, count or proportion for the other backward classes may be asserted from it.
16. **The 2027 enumeration decision and its exact status:** The owners carry the status of the enumeration decision with unusual care as at 21 July 2026: the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 that caste enumeration will be included, the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise, and the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026; the owners then state the boundary, which is that this is an approved and ongoing operation and not published caste data, not a completed methodology and not an automatic basis for revising any list or validating any sub-categorisation formula, so an answer may use the decision as a forthcoming evidence source and must not use it as evidence.
17. **Certificate verification as the administrative weak point of an income-based category:** The owners record that an applicant claiming reservation for the other backward classes must obtain a certificate verifying that the family does not fall within the creamy-layer exclusion, while an applicant for the economically weaker sections category must obtain a certificate from the State or Union Territory authority attesting income and assets, and they identify the second as the harder administrative task because a caste certificate attaches to a hereditary community identity whereas an income certificate must value a flow that is often informal and undocumented; the two recorded failure modes are exclusion of a genuinely poor applicant who cannot document income and fraudulent inclusion of an applicant who conceals it.
18. **Coverage, first-generation access and intergenerational mobility:** The owners separate three things that policy discourse routinely merges: coverage, which counts reservation slots filled and scholarships disbursed; first-generation access, which is what reservation actually delivers, namely entry into higher education or public service for the first member of a family to reach it; and intergenerational mobility, which is realised only if the next generation achieves higher educational attainment and occupational status than the beneficiary, and which stagnates if the children merely re-access the same reservation without progression; the answer-writing consequence is that a recommendation must state which of the three it improves, because an entry instrument does not by itself produce a mobility outcome.
19. **The identification paradox that constrains policy feedback:** The owners state the analytical core of the topic as an identification paradox: reservation for the other backward classes is premised on social and educational backwardness, yet the absence of a published population dataset since 1931 prevents evidence-based calibration of beneficiary proportions, evidence-based sub-categorisation and any cohort-level tracking of mobility, so the policy operates without the feedback loop that would tell it whether it is working; the owners are careful that this is a claim about the evidence base and not a claim that the policy has failed, and an answer should use it to argue for measurement design rather than to assert an outcome that the data cannot support.
20. **The transparent ownership statement for this owner:** The audited routing ledgers, which cover the Mains papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025 and the Prelims papers for 2018 to 2023 and 2024 to 2025, route no General Studies Mains demand and no objective demand to this owner, and both the Basic and the Advanced owner state independently that no confirmed direct 2024 or 2025 General Studies Paper II Mains question on this subject matter exists in the audited local texts, which is consistent with that zero; the 2022 General Studies Paper II demand on the backward-class commission moving from statutory to constitutional status is routed to the Polity commissions owner and the 2018 General Studies Paper II demand on the Scheduled Caste commission and reservation in minority institutions is routed there as well, so neither is claimed here, and the practice in this package is therefore six original Mains questions with model solutions written to the repository's examiner-grade standard together with eighty original objective questions on a strict A → B → C → D rotation.

### OBC, EWS and Social Mobility: CLOSE-OPTION AND ENTITLEMENT TRAPS

- Do not merge the backward-class route with the economic-criterion route, because Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) and Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) are constitutionally distinct enabling provisions.
- Do not say the national commission for backward classes can itself add a community to the Central List, because Article 342A gives that power to the President and Parliament and leaves the commission an examining and advisory role.
- Do not credit the 102nd Amendment with restoring the State power to prepare lists, because the 105th Amendment did that after a judicial reading had removed it.
- Do not call the present commission a statutory body, because its own official page records that it was accorded constitutional status through the 102nd Amendment and that the 1993 Act was repealed in 2018.
- Do not describe the creamy layer as a simple gross-family-income limit, because it rests on notified parental service and status categories and excludes salary and agricultural-land income from the income test.
- Do not apply the creamy-layer exclusion to Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe reservation, because it is confined to the other backward classes.
- Do not state the 1992 judgment as if it created only a percentage, because it also mandated the creamy-layer exclusion and laid down the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling.
- Do not describe the economically weaker sections category as covering economically poor members of the reserved categories, because the definition expressly excludes the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes and the other backward classes.
- Do not quote the economically weaker sections income limit without its asset limits, because the notified test also turns on agricultural land, residential flat area and residential plot area.
- Do not present the 2022 decision as unanimous, because it was a three-to-two majority and the dissent rested on substantive equality.
- Do not treat the two ₹8 lakh figures as one test, because the creamy-layer ceiling and the economically weaker sections ceiling are legally different instruments with different limbs.
- Do not equate the economically backward classes served by the education-support framework with the constitutional economically weaker sections category, because one is a scheme classification and the other is a constitutional route.
- Do not assume any component of the education-support framework is open in every State and year, because the applicable annual guideline decides that.
- Do not state that sub-categorisation has been implemented, because the examining commission of 2017 reported into a process whose implementation remained pending as of mid-2026.
- Do not assert any population share for the other backward classes, because the last published all-caste tables are from 1931 and the 2011 socio-economic and caste data were never released or validated for policy use.
- Do not treat the 2027 enumeration decision as data, because it is an approved and ongoing operation whose first-phase houselisting window ran from 1 April to 30 September 2026 and which has produced no published caste dataset.
- Do not describe an income certificate as administratively equivalent to a caste certificate, because one attests a hereditary community identity and the other must value an often informal and undocumented flow.
- Do not read coverage as mobility, because slots filled and scholarships disbursed measure entry and not intergenerational progression.
- Do not convert the identification paradox into a verdict of policy failure, because it is a statement about the missing evidence base and not about the outcome.
- Do not manufacture a previous-year demand card for this owner, because the audited ledgers route no Mains and no objective demand here and route the two commission demands to the Polity owner.

### OBC, EWS and Social Mobility: ANSWER-WRITING SPINE

```text
NAME THE DEPRIVATION OR CLAIM -> IDENTIFY THE RIGHT-HOLDER AND THE DUTY-BEARER
-> SEPARATE THE CONSTITUTIONAL OR STATUTORY ENTITLEMENT FROM THE SCHEME THAT CARRIES IT
-> TRACE ADMINISTRATIVE DELIVERY: IDENTIFICATION, ENROLMENT, ACCESS, QUALITY
-> TEST THE MEASURED OUTCOME AGAINST THE INPUT, NAMING SURVEY, ROUND AND YEAR
-> SEPARATE INCLUSION ERROR FROM EXCLUSION ERROR AND COVERAGE FROM CAPABILITY
-> CLOSE WITH A GRADED VERDICT AND AN EXPLICIT INDICATOR OR STATUS CAVEAT
```

### OBC, EWS and Social Mobility: DATED-INDICATOR AND STATUS BOUNDARY

One live official page was verified for this topic on 2026-09-02. The National Commission for Backward Classes About Us page on the commission's own website, read on that date, records that the commission was initially constituted by the central government under the National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993 dated 2 April 1993 and had so far been reconstituted seven times up to 2016; that the Act of 1993 has been repealed through the National Commission for Backward Classes (Repeal) Act, 2018 dated 14 August 2018; that the present eighth commission has been accorded constitutional status and was constituted through the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 dated 11 August 2018, whereby Article 338B was inserted, forming a commission for the socially and educationally backward classes to be known as the National Commission for Backward Classes; and that the commission consists of a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and three other Members in the rank and pay of Secretary to the Government of India, whose conditions of service and tenure of office were notified by a Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment notification dated 23 August 2018. Those statements are used only for what they say, and no case, complaint, recommendation, sitting or list-revision figure is inferred from them. Four further official checks were attempted on the same date, namely a departmental scheme identifier that returned a not-found page, a second departmental scheme identifier that returned the same not-found page, a departmental scheme listing page that returned navigational content only and a departmental sector-information page that returned navigational content only, so nothing was read or used from them and no secondary summary, coaching site or news report has been treated as an official source. The package therefore relies on that verified page together with the dated official anchors already carried by the repository owners, each cited with its issuing instrument or decision date: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4) for special provision and reservation; Article 342A for the Central List, with the President specifying and Parliament alone varying it; Article 338B for the national commission; the Constitution (One Hundred and Second Amendment) Act, 2018 and the Constitution (One Hundred and Fifth Amendment) Act, 2021, the second of which restored the power of States to prepare their own lists for State purposes after the 2021 Maratha reservation reading; the Constitution (One Hundred and Third Amendment) Act, 2019 inserting Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) for reservation of up to ten per cent for economically weaker sections outside the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes and the other backward classes; the decision in Indra Sawhney against Union of India of 1992 upholding twenty-seven per cent reservation, mandating the creamy-layer exclusion for the other backward classes only and stating the ordinary fifty per cent ceiling; the creamy-layer income and wealth ceiling of eight lakh rupees notified in September 2017, applied over three consecutive years on top of notified parental service and status categories and with salary and agricultural-land income excluded from simple clubbing; the Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019 setting a gross annual family income below eight lakh rupees together with the asset limits of five acres of agricultural land, a residential flat of 1,000 sq ft, a residential plot of 100 sq yards in a notified municipality and 200 sq yards elsewhere; the decision in Janhit Abhiyan against Union of India of November 2022 upholding that reservation by a majority of three to two, with the recorded majority reasoning and the recorded dissent; the umbrella education-support framework PM-YASASVI of the Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment for students of the other backward classes, the economically backward classes and the denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes; the Rohini Commission of 2017 examining sub-categorisation, with implementation recorded as pending as of mid-2026; the last published all-caste census tables of 1931 and the unreleased and unvalidated caste data of the Socio Economic and Caste Census of 2011; and the recorded status as at 21 July 2026 that the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decided on 30 April 2025 to include caste enumeration, that the 2027 Census is planned as an all-digital exercise and that the first-phase houselisting window was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026. No population share, beneficiary count, certificate count, seat-fill rate, scholarship disbursement, budget or expenditure figure is asserted, no National Family Health Survey, Periodic Labour Force Survey, National Sample Survey, NITI Aayog or Sustainable Development Goal value is asserted, no legal or constitutional provision is invented or upgraded in status, no judicial observation is converted into a binding holding or a legislated rule, no pending report or bill is described as enacted or implemented, and no previous-year question, official answer key or option is asserted or inferred.

### COMPLETE TOPIC ASCII MASTER FLOW DIAGRAM

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 1/12: Two routes, two logics

```ascii-master
DISADVANTAGE -> social and educational backwardness OR pure economic weakness
-> ROUTE A: Article 15(4) and Article 16(4); listed classes; 27 per cent; creamy-layer filter
-> ROUTE B: Article 15(6) and Article 16(6); up to 10 per cent; excludes SC, ST and OBC
-> EVIDENCE A: community backwardness; EVIDENCE B: notified income and asset test
TEST -> neither route supplies the other route's evidence or its filter
MUST REMEMBER: OBC, EWS and social mobility analysis separates social and educational...
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 2/12: Law bank

```ascii-master
ARTICLE 15(4) AND 16(4) -> special provision and reservation for backward classes
ARTICLE 342A -> Central List; President specifies; Parliament alone includes or excludes
ARTICLE 338B -> the national commission for backward classes, inserted in 2018
ARTICLE 15(6) AND 16(6) -> economically weaker sections, inserted in 2019
STATUTE -> National Commission for Backward Classes Act, 1993, later repealed
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 3/12: List-making ladder

```ascii-master
STEP 1 -> community backwardness examined against social and educational indicators
STEP 2 -> commission examines inclusion or exclusion requests and advises
STEP 3 -> President specifies the Central List under Article 342A
STEP 4 -> Parliament alone varies that list by including or excluding a community
PARALLEL -> States prepare their own lists for State purposes after the 105th Amendment
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 4/12: Commission status card

```ascii-master
CONSTITUTED -> under the Act of 1993 dated 2 April 1993; reconstituted seven times to 2016
REPEALED -> the 1993 Act, through the Repeal Act of 2018 dated 14 August 2018
ELEVATED -> eighth commission, 102nd Amendment dated 11 August 2018, Article 338B inserted
COMPOSITION -> Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson and three other Members
RANK -> Secretary to the Government of India; conditions notified on 23 August 2018
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 5/12: Creamy-layer test

```ascii-master
LIMB 1 STATUS -> notified parental service and status categories exclude the applicant
LIMB 2 INCOME -> ceiling of ₹8 lakh applied over three consecutive years
EXCLUSIONS -> salary income and agricultural-land income are not simply clubbed in
SCOPE -> the filter runs inside the other backward classes only
ERROR -> transferring the filter to Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe reservation
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 6/12: Amendment and judgment sequence

```ascii-master
1992 -> Indra Sawhney upholds 27 per cent, mandates creamy layer, states the 50 per cent ceiling
2018 -> 102nd Amendment inserts Article 338B and Article 342A; the 1993 Act is repealed
2019 -> 103rd Amendment inserts Article 15(6) and Article 16(6) for economic weakness
2021 -> Maratha reservation reading centralises list-making; 105th Amendment restores States
2022 -> Janhit Abhiyan upholds the economic criterion by a majority of three to two
CLOSE DISTINCTION: OBC is not a synonym for poverty, EWS is not an OBC sub-category,...
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/12: Economic-criterion card

```ascii-master
NOTIFIED -> Department of Personnel and Training notification dated 31 January 2019
INCOME -> gross annual family income below ₹8 lakh

BUILT -> no residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more
PLOT -> no plot of 100 sq yards in a notified municipality or 200 sq yards elsewhere
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/12: Same figure, different test

```ascii-master
CREAMY LAYER -> ₹8 lakh since September 2017; three consecutive years
PLUS -> notified parental service and status categories; salary and land income excluded
ECONOMIC CRITERION -> ₹8 lakh since January 2019; gross annual family income
PLUS -> notified land, flat and plot asset limits, unchanged since 2019
RULE -> identical arithmetic proves nothing about identical eligibility
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/12: Judgment split

```ascii-master
HELD -> reservation for economically weaker sections is constitutionally valid
MAJORITY -> an economic criterion alone can ground affirmative action
MAJORITY -> exceeding the ceiling for a new category does not breach the basic structure
DISSENT -> excluding the reserved categories from a new benefit denies substantive equality
MARGIN -> three to two, so the disagreement is part of the holding's meaning
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/12: Distribution problem

```ascii-master
QUOTA -> 27 per cent for the other backward classes as a single undivided block
OBSERVED -> concentration of benefit in relatively advanced communities within the block
PROPOSAL -> sub-categorisation of the quota among sub-groups
EXAMINED BY -> the commission set up in 2017; implementation pending as of mid-2026
REQUIREMENT -> backwardness and representation data for each sub-group
```

```ascii-master
LAST PUBLISHED ALL-CASTE TABLES -> 1931
SOCIO-ECONOMIC AND CASTE CENSUS 2011 -> caste data never released or validated for policy
DECISION -> Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs, 30 April 2025, to enumerate caste
OPERATION -> all-digital 2027 Census; houselisting notified 1 April to 30 September 2026
```

```ascii-master

MOBILITY -> the next generation exceeding the beneficiary in education and occupation
STAGNATION -> children re-accessing the same reservation without progression
```
